**Operational implications.** (1) The marker table must exist **before** this code runs, or every login answers the generic 500 (fail closed; no session). (2) Old and new login code never share a challenge: there is no fallback, so a rolling deployment must not mix them, and rotating `REAL_SESSION_SECRET` invalidates outstanding challenges. (3) Rollback: roll the application code back first; do **not** delete or truncate markers; the table can be dropped only after the code no longer uses it. (4) Marker rows are written only for successfully verified logins and are purged at most 64 per marker statement once expired for over an hour, so growth is bounded by successful logins, not by anonymous traffic. (5) `login/options` can no longer cause durable writes.

**Residuals (read this).** This is **not** a rate limiter: there is no global quota, no per-IP or per-source control, and no trusted-ingress attribution. `login/verify` is still unlimited and, for a validly signed token with a credential id, still performs database reads (the passkey lookup) before verification; a replayed *valid* assertion is stopped by the marker but still costs that work. Login-driven provisioning advisory-lock contention was untouched by 3C-2; Slice 3C-3a (below) later reduces re-entry into the claim on exhausted refusal paths only and does not eliminate advisory-lock acquisition, row-lock contention, or anonymous database amplification. The pre-existing authenticator sign-counter update remains a blind write and is a separate, unaddressed issue. `test/lib/real/login-stateless.test.ts` has a future-dated known-answer challenge whose behavior can change in a narrow January 2027 window (a test-quality issue, not a vulnerability; recorded for a separate pass). The 3C-1 residuals (anonymous exhaustion of the registration pool, no retention cleanup for registration attempts, trusted ingress, 3C-3, 3D, 3E, real-device registration compatibility, slow-upload/transport denial of service, the unresolved deployment prerequisites, and the unapplied provider-budget migration) are unchanged. The overall Milestone 3 remains open, Milestone 2 remains DEFERRED, and **public deployment remains NOT AUTHORIZED**.

### Public Readiness Milestone 3C — Slice 3C-3a: Advisory Read-Only Provisioning Pre-Check, "O1-A" (CLOSED at `beac686`; live shape-checked on ONE disposable branch; NOT deployable)

Milestone 3C is "Unauthenticated Rate Limits"; 3C-1 and 3C-2 are CLOSED. **3C-3a is the first part of 3C-3** (CLOSED at `beac686`); 3C-3b (canonical registration-challenge shape validation) has since been built and is CLOSED at `48a98d9` (see "Slice 3C-3b" below), and 3C-3c is not authorized; 3D (provider timeouts) and 3E (authenticated write limits) are not started, Milestone 2 stays DEFERRED, no milestone is renumbered, and **3C-3 is not complete and Milestone 3 as a whole is not closed**. Keep these statuses distinct:

- *Technical implementation:* COMPLETE. *Independent implementation security audit* and *independent whole-slice security audit:* **PASSED** (0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium; two Low findings, below). *Controlled PostgreSQL shape check:* one run, **`53b7f63e`, `completed_clean`**. *Independent evidence audit:* **READY FOR TECHNICAL ACCEPTANCE AND DOCUMENTATION CLOSEOUT** (no Critical, High or Medium; one Low evidence-precision issue, below). *Control room:* technical implementation accepted.
- *Formal slice closure:* **CLOSED.** The operator's manual Git checkpoint is `beac68660fe3574104583148a057352fd72331c3` ("feat: add advisory provisioning dispatch precheck", verified against `git log`; 3 production files, 2 modified tests and 1 new test file on top of `7d66abe`). Closure is not a deployment approval: it is not "3C closed", not "Milestone 3 closed", not production-ready, not deployed, and not applied to the application database (which has also not received the pending 3C-1 and 3C-2 migrations).
- *3C-2 closure:* Slice 3C-2 is CLOSED at `7d66abe` (see its section); this slice does not reopen it.

**Design.** A new store method, `peekProvisioningDispatchRefusal({ credentialId })`, is an **advisory, read-only** question asked once at the very start of `runProvisioningDispatch` — before Turnkey dependency resolution, any application-clock read, request-body construction, the request-body SHA-256, and the authoritative locked claim. It reports one of `proceed`, `attempt_budget_exhausted`, or `global_capacity_reached`. Only the two exhaustion outcomes can end the request early, and they return the pipeline's **existing** outcomes (`attempt_budget_exhausted`, `capacity_reached`), so the existing 409 `registration_setup_exhausted` and 503 `registration_capacity_reached` responses are unchanged; no `Retry-After` was introduced. **`proceed` grants nothing.** The original locked claim, `beginProvisioningDispatch`, remains the **sole dispatch admission gate** and is unchanged (byte-identical to HEAD; function-body SHA-256 `58e4ad6f00dfbf7667d75ccc4a81d4e9152cce0491bebbc5af889555cfaf8e7a`): the attempt-row lock, the transaction-scoped `OCF_PROV` advisory lock, READ COMMITTED, the CAS plus ledger insert, the original result precedence, and sole authority over sending to the provider with no blind retry. The M1 limits are unchanged (5 per rolling hour, 20 per rolling 24 hours, 2 per attempt). **No table, counter, index, column or migration was added** and `schema.sql` is untouched. The pipeline (`onboarding.ts`), both verify routes, and both callers — registration verification and the pending-registration login resume — are unchanged; the active-passkey login path never reaches the pre-check.

**Query and failure semantics.** On Neon it is **one autocommit `SELECT`** (not a batch, no explicit transaction, no isolation override), with no `FOR UPDATE`-style clause, no advisory-lock acquisition, no write and no DDL. (An ordinary statement still takes PostgreSQL's usual shared table locks; this is not "zero locking".) It reads the database clock once through a materialized CTE (`clock_timestamp()`), computes the claim's materialized `ledger` CTE and its `CASE` precedence — strict `created_at > cutoff`, every ledger row counted whatever its terminal status, future-dated rows counted, the same M1 constants as bound parameters, the same inconsistency precedence (lost claim, inconsistent ledger, attempt budget, global capacity, otherwise possible) — and a test compares the CTE and `CASE` text with the claim's byte for byte. Mapping: `claim_lost`, `ledger_inconsistent` and `admit` all become `proceed` (the claim reports or decides them; a ledger inconsistency is never hidden behind a refusal); `attempt_budget_exhausted` and `global_capacity_reached` pass through. **A failed read, no row, two rows, or any unexpected decision throws**, and `runProvisioningDispatch` throws on any answer that is not one of the three outcomes; there is no fallback to dispatch, and the route answers its generic 500. The in-memory store implements the same decision on its injected clock, writes nothing, and never returns an admission.

**Accepted concurrency tradeoff (read this).** The pre-check runs without the claim's locks, so it can be stale in either direction. (1) *Stale refusal (false denial):* at T1 it can observe a full shared window and return the existing capacity refusal although the claim would have admitted at some later T2 because an older dispatch aged out of the rolling window. The size of this window is bounded by the time gap between the pre-check's evaluation and the claim's evaluation — **not a guaranteed fixed delay**; that gap can include time the claim spends waiting for its own locks. Nothing is changed by such a refusal: the attempt stays `verified` and is resumable through the existing later login/resume; no automatic retry was added. The operator accepted this tradeoff. (2) *Stale message:* two requests for the same attempt may race, so an early 503 or 409 can be returned after a sibling changed the attempt's state, where the claim would instead have returned `claim_lost` and re-read the new state. No unauthorized dispatch occurs and the pre-check makes no durable change. (3) *Stale `proceed`:* simply re-decided by the claim, which refuses if capacity filled meanwhile (tested).

**Actual benefit — and what this is not.** While capacity is exhausted, one advisory read-only query replaces repeated entry into the claim's locked transaction: it avoids the claim's row lock, the global advisory lock, and request-body construction on an early refusal. When capacity is available, **one extra database read** now precedes each eligible dispatch. The global-window counts still scan the dispatch ledger, and **no `created_at` index was added**. **This is not a rate limiter** and does not make 3C-3 complete. It does not stop anonymous `login/verify` reads (the passkey lookup), anonymous `register/verify` challenge-consume `DELETE`s, login-marker writes, authenticator-counter updates, or general database flooding. It does not eliminate advisory-lock acquisition or row-lock contention: it only **reduces re-entry into the claim on exhausted refusal paths**. Trusted-edge, per-source abuse protection remains unresolved and is a prerequisite for public registration.

**Offline verification.** 43 new tests in `test/lib/real/provisioning-dispatch-precheck.test.ts` (per-attempt, hourly and daily refusal; strict `>` at ±1 ms for both windows; future-dated rows; the attempt budget outranking global capacity; an 80-round property test that the pre-check refuses exactly when the claim refuses and that `proceed` never admits; a stale `proceed` and a stale refusal; read failure, `null` and unknown answers failing closed with no claim and no provider call; call order pre-check → clock → claim; consumption/counter/pre-check ordering for the pending-login branch and a replay never reaching it; the active-passkey path never reaching it; unchanged 503/409/500 mapping; the Neon statement shape and its byte comparison with the claim; the `OCF_PROV` lock remaining only in the claim) and two narrow amendments in existing tests (the store method list gained the new read-only method; the "one claim attempt per run on a refusal" test became two: the pre-check refuses first and the claim is not attempted, and on `proceed` the claim is attempted once and refuses). Mutation checks (removing the pre-check call, flipping the boundary to `>=`, swapping the `CASE` order) each failed the intended tests and were restored. The independent audits reconciled the full-suite runs to **5,455 passing and 370 skipped**; this was **not one uninterrupted single green full-suite run**. A `session-security` balance-route test timed out under suite load once and passed alone (a known unrelated load flake), and a color-sensitive harness self-test was isolated and resolved for verification with `NO_COLOR=1`. No unrelated test was weakened. `tsc`, ESLint and `git diff --check` were clean.

**Controlled PostgreSQL shape check — run `53b7f63e`, `completed_clean`.** One run on the disposable Neon branch `ocf-m3c3a-peek-shape-01` (branch id `br-bitter-feather-b5hnx52u`, endpoint `ep-withered-hill-b5ewmitm`, parent `production` / `br-wandering-hat-b5w7je3x`); its **parent relationship and 7-day expiration are OPERATOR-CONFIRMED through the Neon Console and were not proved by SQL**. The run used the new disposable pooled endpoint, never the application database. PostgreSQL **18.6**, **READ COMMITTED**. The **actual production adapter** was invoked once with a nonexistent synthetic credential id and returned **`{ outcome: "proceed" }`** with exactly one single statement and six bound parameters; the raw server decision was `claim_lost` (mapped to `proceed`) with PostgreSQL type `text`. Seven database statements were sent in total, all within the read-only scope: five plain `SELECT` diagnostics (identity/isolation, table existence, counts before, the `pg_typeof` type query, counts after), the adapter's own `SELECT`, and one `EXPLAIN` **without `ANALYZE`** (a 26-line plan). The pre-check statement therefore executed twice (via the adapter, and inside the diagnostic type query). Row counts: `registration_attempts` 3 before / 3 after; `registration_provisioning_dispatches` 1 before / 1 after. No data-changing SQL or DDL appears in the executed statement inventory; no retries, SQLSTATE errors, unknown or in-flight operations were recorded. This was one controlled run, not repeated smoke testing. Two local runner-preparation failures and one import-only dry run happened before the single live execution and made no database connection. The check establishes that PostgreSQL **parses** the statement, accepts its **parameter typing**, returns the expected **row shape**, and that the adapter's **mapping** works — nothing more.

**Evidence files** (private, mode 0600 in a 0700 directory, git-ignored under `/evidence/`; independently audited; contain no connection URL, password, signing secret or raw account data; never modify, move, copy, commit or paste their contents): `evidence/peek-shape-53b7f63e-start.json`, `evidence/peek-shape-53b7f63e-final.json`.

**Evidence-precision notes (from the Low evidence finding; none changes the accepted clean result).** (a) The start evidence says the SQL was statically checked before dispatch; for the actual production-adapter statement the check was applied **after** dispatch, on the captured text, and the SQL was independently reviewed by the Auditor. (b) The evidence says the 3C-2 digest was recomputed immediately before execution; the runner carried the value from the prior offline recomputation, and the evidence auditor recomputed it again afterwards (`681b7c4ebc0a714ac80d7d3a5d38b4c894d4258e3e53ddb5b85d301a6c871edc`, unchanged). That digest covers selected dependencies, not the whole repository. (c) `writes: 0`, `ddl: 0`, `transactions: 0` are **runner-reported constants, not independent measurements**; `transactions: 0` means no explicit transaction batch, not no implicit PostgreSQL transaction. (d) Matching row counts are not a database-object diff. (e) The raw decision `claim_lost` came from the second (diagnostic) execution, not from a direct capture of the adapter's own server response.

**Accepted Low findings.** *Low-1:* the development-only in-memory ledger-inconsistency branch has no direct test with an actually inconsistent ledger (the in-memory API cannot construct one; the mapping is tested at the Neon wire level and with a stub). Accepted as nonblocking. *Low-2:* the SQL had not run against PostgreSQL when the offline audits ran; the controlled shape check above later covered its parsing, parameter handling, row shape and adapter mapping — it does not remove the concurrency or performance unknowns.

**Remaining unverified (do not call these proven).** The live per-attempt refusal branch, the live hourly and daily capacity refusals, the live `ledger_inconsistent` path; concurrent dispatch behavior under production-like load; any measured reduction in lock contention; latency or performance; exact database-object state beyond the measured row counts; real-device passkey behavior; trusted-edge per-source admission; the application database's migration readiness (the 3C-1 and 3C-2 migrations are still not applied there); and public deployment readiness. **The branch `ocf-m3c3a-peek-shape-01` is spent for this purpose and must not be casually reused**; no branch deletion and no new live SQL is authorized.

**Boundary note, not resolved here.** An earlier 3C-3 scope audit noted a possible discrepancy between documentation describing a 10 MiB global request-body ceiling and the source's 256 KiB `REAL_API_MAX_BODY_BYTES`. That is a separate request-body policy/documentation review; this closeout neither changes nor decides it.

### Public Readiness Milestone 3C — Slice 3C-3b: Canonical Registration Challenge Shape Validation, "A-E2" (CLOSED at `48a98d9`; independent whole-slice audit PASSED; NOT deployable)

Milestone 3C is "Unauthenticated Rate Limits"; 3C-1, 3C-2 and 3C-3a are CLOSED (`46d1cc2`, `7d66abe`, `beac686`). **3C-3b is the second part of 3C-3.** 3C-3c is not authorized, 3D (provider timeouts) and 3E (authenticated write limits) are not started, Milestone 2 stays DEFERRED, no milestone is renumbered, and **3C-3 is not complete, 3C is open, and Milestone 3 as a whole is not closed**. Keep these statuses distinct:

- *Technical implementation:* COMPLETE. *Independent implementation security audit:* PASSED. *Post-audit test-quality correction:* complete (one Low and three small hygiene items, below). *Final independent whole-slice security audit:* **PASSED** — 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 0 Low, 8 Informational; verdict "ready for technical acceptance and documentation closeout"; no implementation correction required. *Control room:* technical implementation accepted. No live PostgreSQL verification was needed or run: no schema, adapter or consume SQL changed.
- *Formal slice closure:* **CLOSED at `48a98d9`** ("feat: harden registration challenge shape before consumption", the operator's manual Git checkpoint, verified against `git log`). This entry's earlier wording that the work was uncommitted working-tree changes and that closure was pending is **historical and superseded**. It is not "3C-3 complete", not "3C closed", not "Milestone 3 closed", not production-ready, not deployed, and not applied to the application database (which has also not received the 3C-1 and 3C-2 migrations).

**What it is — and is not.** Partial **input-shape hardening**: a registration challenge that is not shaped like one this server could have issued is refused before the database is asked about it, which avoids a pointless `DELETE` for obviously invalid input. **It is NOT a rate limiter.** Canonical random challenges are trivial to generate, so a well-formed bogus challenge still reaches the database. The validation also does not prove that a challenge was actually issued; only the unchanged consume does.

**Design.** One private, pure helper in `lib/real/server/registration.ts`, `isCanonicalRegistrationChallenge` (not exported; no new dependency, no I/O). It accepts a value only if it is a primitive string, exactly 43 characters, drawn only from `A-Z a-z 0-9 - _` (no padding), decodes to exactly 32 bytes, and **re-encodes to exactly the original string**. The final equality check rejects non-canonical trailing spare bits, which lenient decoders accept (the 43rd character carries two spare bits; for example 42 `A`s followed by `B` decodes to the same 32 zero bytes as 43 `A`s). The cheap type, length and alphabet checks run before any decoding, so an oversized or badly-shaped value is never decoded. The existing unknown-challenge reason text became a named constant (`UNKNOWN_REGISTRATION_CHALLENGE`) with its wording unchanged.

**Verify side (`completeRegistration`).** Order: (1) decode `clientDataJSON`; (2) validate the challenge shape; (3) consume the challenge through the original challenge store; (4) verify the WebAuthn attestation; (5) the original registration, finalization and provisioning flow. A malformed challenge returns the **existing unknown-challenge rejection** (`rejected`, the same reason string), so the verify route answers the same HTTP 400 body it gives for an unknown, expired or used challenge and reveals nothing about whether a challenge exists. It is rejected before any challenge-store, registry, registration-attempt or provider work. A malformed `clientDataJSON` keeps its own existing "Malformed registration response." rejection. A canonical but nonexistent challenge still reaches the original consume exactly once, with the `registration` purpose.

**Options side (`beginRegistration`).** After the library generates the options, `optionsJSON.challenge` is checked with the **same** validator before `challengeStore.create` and before anything is returned. A non-canonical generated challenge throws: it is not normalized, not stored and not returned, and the route answers its existing generic HTTP 500. The 3C-1 admission may already have been charged for that start and **is not refunded or changed**. No new database operation or durable state exists. The installed SimpleWebAuthn is 14.0.2; with no challenge supplied it mints 32 random bytes encoded as 43 canonical unpadded base64url characters, so the options-side check is a fail-closed guard against a future library or configuration change, not a change to what is issued today.

**Replay and compatibility.** The atomic `DELETE ... RETURNING` in the Neon store's `consume` is unchanged and still the only one-time guarantee; no read-before-delete was added, and consumption still precedes attestation verification. A consumed challenge cannot be reused, a failed attestation still consumes it, and concurrent verifications cannot both consume the same challenge. The offline concurrency test uses the in-memory store; **production atomicity rests on the unchanged PostgreSQL statement and PostgreSQL concurrency was not retested for this slice.** No legitimate challenge format changed: previously issued, still-outstanding registration challenges came from the same unchanged generation path. WebAuthn attestation validation and the attested credential-ID binding are unchanged. Pending-registration recovery uses the 3C-2 login challenge and the provisioning pipeline, not registration-challenge consumption, and is unchanged. Safe ownership, Turnkey authority, signing, the M1 locked claim, the 3C-1 admission, the 3C-2 login challenges, the 3C-3a pre-check and Practice Mode are untouched.

**Tests.** New `test/lib/real/registration-challenge-shape.test.ts`, **51 tests**, run through the real `beginRegistration` / `completeRegistration` entry points and the real verify and options routes with in-memory stores, the fake parent Turnkey and mocked route dependencies. Coverage: non-string, missing, null, array and object challenges; 42 and 44 characters; padding; the wrong alphabet; whitespace, tab, newline and NUL; Unicode; 1 MiB and 200 KB values (the cheap checks run before any `atob`); non-canonical trailing spare bits, proved to be accepted by a lenient decoder so that only the re-encode check refuses them; issued challenges accepted; a valid registration completing; a well-formed unknown challenge reaching consume exactly once; zero consume, create, registry, attempt and provider calls for every malformed case; the byte-identical rejection for malformed and unknown challenges, including through the route; replay, failed-attestation burn and three-way concurrency (in memory); the options side failing before `create` with the generic 500; and source-shape guards on the consume call and ordering. Mutation checks removing the verify-side check, dropping the re-encode equality, loosening the alphabet pattern, removing the options-side check and altering the byte length each failed intended tests and were restored byte for byte; dropping only the explicit length check failed nothing because the pattern's `{43}` already enforces length (an equivalent mutant; the explicit check is cheap defense in depth). **Post-audit correction:** the suite originally contained a test that ran Git to check which production files had changed; the independent audit flagged it Low (it depends on the developer's working-tree state and would fail on legitimate future work). It was **removed** and not replaced; scope verification belongs to the audit workflow. Three small hygiene items were also done and verified by the final audit: an explicit spare-bit test value replacing a confusing ternary, a case of exactly 43 characters with a newline as the final character, and a reset of the hoisted route-test state before every test.

**Request-body fixture amendment.** `test/lib/real/request-body-limits.test.ts` used a 26-character registration challenge that the server could never have issued, so its "ordinary valid request reaches the registration service" cases began failing once the shape check existed. The fixture challenge was changed to a canonical 43-character value, `"A".repeat(43)`, with an explanatory comment. It is a shape-valid placeholder, not a genuinely issued challenge. No body-size limit, threshold, error contract or assertion was weakened, and the request-body implementation is unchanged.

**Verification record (kept distinct; do not add together).** The Builder's complete offline run before the test cleanup was **5,504 passed / 1 failed / 370 skipped**; the one failure was the previously known same-millisecond "FIX 3" ordering flake in `backup-step-up-and-pending-removal.test.ts`, which the Builder reproduced in isolation (it failed on several isolated runs, failed the same way with `registration.ts` temporarily reverted to HEAD, and its test file does not import `registration.ts`). An earlier Builder run had also failed two `request-body-limits` cases until the fixture was corrected. The Builder's related-suite run after the cleanup (new suite plus `request-body-limits`, `registration`, `registration-admission-route`, `provisioning-dispatch-precheck`, `login-stateless`) was 582 passed. The independent whole-slice audit then reported the **51-test new suite passing and 633 combined targeted offline tests passing**, with TypeScript and ESLint clean, and reproduced the FIX 3 flake in isolation. **There was no single uninterrupted fully green full-suite run, and none is claimed.** All verification is offline; no SQL, provider call, migration or deployment was involved.

**Audited source identities** (SHA-256, recorded by the independent whole-slice audit; the Builder re-verified them before and after this documentation pass): `lib/real/server/registration.ts` `655f3408b9764f84a76af24b2a5d2f7d4a9087b910181e4c4f38d122928ba50d`; `test/lib/real/request-body-limits.test.ts` `4c5525d02384b3b0f2554e0ab498f243feeda01eb5469e44aa2972bc736b25a6`; `test/lib/real/registration-challenge-shape.test.ts` `71fb82e9888ed4bc3eba371249150c8cba755049fcb8a39d76c4845a5e473d1d`. `schema.sql`, the Neon store, the provisioning-dispatch module and the registration-attempt store are unchanged from HEAD; the M1 claim (function-body SHA-256 `58e4ad6f…8e7a`) and the 3C-2 selected-source digest (`681b7c4e…1edc`) inputs are therefore unchanged. The final audit established that source integrity by confirming the relevant claim and store files were identical to HEAD; **it did not independently recompute the M1 function hash.** The historical migration pins (Login Challenge Uses 15070 / `00ff3969…`, Registration Admission 13101 / `b269ebcc…`, Handle Pay Rate Limits 11680 / `78c5557d…`, Provider Budgets 11820 / `8213b46c…`) are untouched.

**Audit informational findings worth keeping (none required a correction).** (A) Some previously malformed inputs, which would have reached the database and produced a database-derived 500, now return the unknown-challenge 400 earlier; the prior database-error behavior was inferred, not measured live. (B) The consume `DELETE` keys on the challenge value alone: purpose and expiry are checked **after** the row is deleted, so a wrong-purpose or expired challenge is still consumed (pre-existing behavior, not introduced or changed here; the 3C-3b check does not alter it). (C) PostgreSQL concurrency was not retested for this slice. (D) The known "FIX 3" flake remains. (E) The full offline suite was not entirely green in one run. The remaining three Informational items were minor test-structure observations (including the shape-valid placeholder fixture) and are not separate architectural warnings.

**Remaining abuse exposure — do not read this slice as closing any of it.** *E1:* unlimited junk `login/verify` requests still cause indexed passkey lookups. *E2 (residual):* canonical random registration challenges are cheap to generate and each still causes the original `DELETE`. *E3:* repeated valid logins with an attacker-controlled passkey still cause repeated database writes (the login marker and the authenticator sign-counter update). The shared 3C-1 registration-start pool (30 per hour, 100 per day) remains exhaustible by anonymous callers. Trusted per-source attribution is not established. A **global anonymous login limit remains rejected**: an attacker could exhaust it and deny legitimate login and pending-registration recovery. The application database still needs the separately planned 3C-1 and 3C-2 migrations, and **public deployment remains NOT AUTHORIZED**.

**Not decided or touched here:** the 10 MiB versus 256 KiB request-gate documentation review; Milestone 2 invitation-only registration; 3D provider timeouts; 3E authenticated write limits; trusted-edge implementation; credential-ID shape restrictions on `login/verify`; E3 per-credential budgeting; Safe ownership, Turnkey authority and signing infrastructure; Practice Mode.

### Bounded Shared WebAuthn Challenge Purge — a preparatory hardening slice associated with future Milestone 3E (technical proof ACCEPTED; live-verified on ONE disposable Neon branch; documentation audit and the operator's manual Git checkpoint PENDING; NOT deployable)

**Numbering.** This is a narrow preparatory slice. It is **not** Milestone 3E ("authenticated write limits"), which is not started, and it renumbers nothing: M0 and M1 are CLOSED, M2 stays DEFERRED, M3A, M3B, M3C-1, M3C-2, M3C-3a and M3C-3b are CLOSED, and **M3C overall, M3D (provider timeouts), the broader M3E and Milestone 3 as a whole are all still open or not started.** Keep these statuses distinct:

- *Production implementation:* COMPLETE; the independent whole-slice security audit of the implementation PASSED. The Builder's offline verification was **686 passed / 11 skipped / 0 failed across 9 files** (the skips are the gated live cases and the confirmation-print test). The unrelated Practice `Date.now()` race and the "FIX 3" ordering flake live in test files that were not among those 9.
- *Live-proof harness:* built offline, then audited three times (the last, final harness audit: 0 Critical / 0 High / 0 Medium / 0 Low / 8 Informational). A separate audit of the controlled Neon target passed, **with provenance as reported by the operator**, and an offline confirmation for run `226ecfaa` passed (62 passed / 21 skipped / 0 failed).
- *Live PostgreSQL proof:* run **`226ecfaa`**, October 24, 2026, base/correctness mode (PERF OFF) on ONE disposable Neon branch — outcome **`completed_clean`**; Vitest 70 passed / 13 skipped / 0 failed; **P1-P9 all passed**.
- *Independent live-evidence audit:* 0 Critical / 0 High / 0 Medium / **1 accepted nonblocking Low** (the observed plan, below) / 7 Informational; verdict "ready for documentation-only closeout". No further PostgreSQL SQL is required for technical acceptance.
- *Formal slice closure:* **PENDING** this documentation's independent audit and the operator's manual Git checkpoint. At this writing the six slice files are **uncommitted working-tree changes on top of `48a98d9`** (4 modified tracked files, 2 untracked); no commit for this slice is recorded, and nothing is claimed closed, deployed or production-ready.

**What changed.** `createNeonChallengeStore.create` used to delete **every** expired WebAuthn challenge before inserting the new one: `WITH purged AS (DELETE FROM webauthn_challenges WHERE expires_at < $1) INSERT ...`. It is still ONE autocommit statement, with the purge and the insert together, but the purge is now a **bounded candidate selection**:

```sql
WITH purged AS (
  DELETE FROM webauthn_challenges d
  WHERE d.challenge IN (
    SELECT p.challenge FROM webauthn_challenges p
    WHERE p.expires_at < $1
    ORDER BY p.expires_at LIMIT 64 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED
  )
)
INSERT INTO webauthn_challenges (challenge, purpose, context, expires_at) VALUES (...)
```

Semantics, each preserved from the old statement unless stated: at most **64 expired rows deleted per create**, **oldest expiry first**, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (concurrent creates skip rows another create holds instead of waiting); the cutoff is still the **application clock with a strict less-than** (the same clock that wrote `expires_at` and that `consume` checks, so nothing `consume` would still accept is purged); the purge is purpose-independent, so **all six purposes share it**; the INSERT arguments, result handling, TTL and error propagation are unchanged, and a database error still propagates with no retry. An expired row left behind is never consumable (`consume` still checks expiry itself), so deferring cleanup extends no challenge's validity; a backlog drains over later creates, 64 at a time. The in-memory store matches it (oldest `expiresAt` first, at most 64 per create). **No migration, new index, or schema change was needed or made**, and `schema.sql` is untouched.

**Unchanged, and don't weaken:** the atomic `DELETE ... RETURNING` consume and its replay safety (consumption still precedes attestation verification; a wrong-purpose or expired challenge is still burned); challenge purposes and contexts; the challenge format and random generation; registration, login, recovery, Safe ownership and Turnkey authority. **The 3C-2 stateless login challenges and their one-time marker purge are untouched** and keep their different, deliberate semantics (a one-hour retention margin and the database clock); the 3C-1 admission accounting is untouched. The 3C-2 harness test received a one-line, test-only correction (its "production adapter" digest mutation now targets an anchored `interval '1 hour'` clause, because this slice added an earlier `LIMIT 64` to `neon-store.ts`); the 3C-2 selected-source digest `681b7c4e...` is unaffected.

**What it is not.** It is **not** an HTTP rate limiter, an admission limiter, or an authenticated-write budget. It caps the **expired rows deleted per create**, not the number of creates and not all database work associated with them (see the plan limitation below). Authenticated challenge creation remains unbounded.

**Live evidence (run `226ecfaa`, October 24, 2026).** Target: Neon project `onchain-finance-dev`, branch `ocf-m3e-purge-proof-01` (branch id `br-cold-butterfly-b583hg4p`), compute endpoint `ep-rough-hall-b5r5cdrs`, database `neondb`, role `neondb_owner`; created **schema-only** from the `production` branch (source branch id `br-wandering-hat-b5w7je3x`). **That parentage and creation mode are the operator's Neon Console confirmation, not independent provider proof.** Two git-ignored private files, never to be edited or committed: `evidence/challenge-purge-smoke-226ecfaa-start.json` and `evidence/challenge-purge-smoke-226ecfaa-final.json` (the independent audit found both regular files at mode 0600 in a 0700 directory, valid JSON, the right run identity, source digest and target fingerprints, no apparent credentials, and start and final consistent). Harness-observed database outcome `cleanup_confirmed`; no uncertain writes, no refusals, no unresolved outstanding SQL, no client deadline fired, no retry. Budgets actually used: **109 SQL operations of 400; 65 writes; 1,353 scratch rows of 6,000; 9 public run-owned values of 10; maximum concurrency 8 of a 16 ceiling; one scratch schema; cleanup within its 24-statement ceiling.** Cleanup was assessed from the persisted harness evidence, **not from a new post-run SQL connection**: the public table returned to 0 rows through an exact-value delete of only the registered run-owned values, the owned scratch table and then schema were dropped with `RESTRICT` (never `CASCADE`), the schema listing matched the baseline, and both evidence saves succeeded.

**What P1-P9 showed.** *P1:* the unchanged production adapter worked against the disposable public table; parameter binding and the JSONB context round trip passed. *P2:* with 100 expired scratch rows, one create deleted the oldest 64 and left 36. *P3:* all six purposes were handled the same way and live rows survived. *P4:* the 64, 65 and 160-row backlog boundaries behaved correctly, and sequential creates deleted at most 64 each. *P5:* the cutoff is a strict less-than, including at microsecond precision. *P6:* eight concurrent creates over a 600-row expired backlog all inserted, the six live rows were preserved, 512 expired rows were deleted and 88 remained, with no errors or deadlocks — **an aggregate concurrency assertion, not an independent per-statement deletion-count proof**. *P7:* with a real PostgreSQL lock holder keeping the oldest 64 rows locked, the purge skipped them and deleted the next eligible rows **without waiting** for the holder to release. *P8:* an expired challenge did not authenticate, and a live challenge was consumed exactly once across concurrent attempts, with the replay failing. *P9:* `EXPLAIN` **without** `ANALYZE` showed the expected plan structure; it is **not** a performance benchmark.

**Accepted Low — the observed plan does not bound total work.** P9's observed plan on the small fixture used an index scan to select the expiry-ordered candidates, with `Limit` and `LockRows`, and an outer `DELETE` implemented as a **Hash Join whose build side was a sequential scan of the delete target**. So the 64-row **deletion** cap does **not** guarantee bounded **scanning or locking** work, and this slice does not prevent database-level write amplification or denial of service. That plan is a small-fixture plan, **not a production-scale measurement**: do not assume every future plan repeats the full scan, and do not assume it will not. Production-scale latency, scan cost, plan stability, dead tuples and contention are **unmeasured**. This is recorded as an explicit **nonblocking residual**, not silently resolved; the SQL was deliberately not changed in this slice.

**Other accepted limitations (stated plainly; none is an invented blocker).** (A) P6 verifies aggregate deletion under concurrency, not each statement's count; the per-statement bound rests on the SQL's own `LIMIT 64` and on the separate inspection of the production statement. (B) The harness's deadlines are client-side and do not prove server-side statement cancellation. (C) Cleanup rests on harness-observed readback and independently audited evidence; no separate post-run database inspection was made. (D) Real-device passkey compatibility was not tested (no ceremony ran). (E) PERF mode was not executed. (F) P9 is not a latency benchmark. (G) The 64-row cap does not bound all scans or locks. (H) The legacy `neon-smoke.test.ts` "S4" smoke assumes one create purges its expired fixture row; with more than 64 older expired rows it can **false-fail**. It was not edited and must not be run as part of this proof; its compatibility needs a separate decision. (I) The executed proof harness header still says "IT HAS NEVER BEEN RUN LIVE." That was true before execution and is **superseded by run `226ecfaa`**; the executed file is preserved byte for byte and its wording is deliberately not edited. (J) The target lineage is the operator's Console confirmation, not independent provider identity verification. (K) The slice does not resolve broader authenticated write amplification. Real Mode remains testnet-only, and this slice does not make the product public- or mainnet-ready.

**Branch policy.** The branch `ocf-m3e-purge-proof-01` and run id `226ecfaa` are **SPENT**, even though the run was clean: never reuse either, and never point another run at that branch. No branch deletion is authorized here; disposal is a separate operator decision. No further SQL against it is required or authorized.

**Source identities (SHA-256; the Builder re-verified them before and after this documentation pass).** `lib/real/server/neon-store.ts` `e249b263f8a5e042bb90b0cb753730a936e278199e7b173e43c68dbc2c890f9a`; `lib/real/server/challenge-store.ts` `532a7b64b9530b0f9a5b840bfcb9a025e1b1d5927ccc0da8e00d6a11867be486`; `test/lib/real/challenge-store.test.ts` `98a49db989cb2150624d48252a477a2d2b0c5fcc6b4846cf74201a29dbe0be25`; `test/lib/real/challenge-store-bounded-purge.test.ts` `cd048d6fa96a25b8d9e1fbd3a2977e40c4faebb6ceb1ea29474cf3dd30532c34`; `test/lib/real/login-challenge-uses-smoke-harness.test.ts` `23ebfe28b017ab7299fd1c7a6b9001d80dc7506f6bda1d97a70072c0eacc283c`; and the executed proof harness `test/lib/real/challenge-store-bounded-purge-neon-proof.test.ts` `347fd0e078d43dafcac4369bfcb858beb0291b0bffcdf1acb21fb476c8fc8b55`. The audited live source digest (as recorded by the independent evidence audit) is `f790b5ca7aa2cb07e12d9c2bdaf28325d5e1115bfbd027aba6029e3781e35791`. The protected M3C-2 selected-source digest remains `681b7c4ebc0a714ac80d7d3a5d38b4c894d4258e3e53ddb5b85d301a6c871edc`. Do not edit these files, or the evidence, without a new decision and a fresh audit.

## Explicitly Not Yet Decided

- How authentication and accounts work beyond a testnet account identity and its handle (Phase 2/3)
- End-to-end testing framework (not yet justified by current surface area)
- Provider-request budgets: the production numeric limits and windows for all six buckets (Milestone 3; the chain-read, pay-submit and turnkey-reconcile values are development values only, and provider pricing and quotas are unresearched)
- The global request-body ceiling for anything beyond controlled local development. `REAL_API_MAX_BODY_BYTES` is 256 KiB (262 144) for controlled development, set by the separate post-3B change (Milestone 3B itself closed with 10 MiB); every one of the 22 body routes is bounded by its own smaller limit. Reconsidering it (128 KiB was floated once and not chosen) needs a separate policy decision covering actual request shapes, hosting and CDN behavior, real-device registration payloads, concurrent-upload memory and transport-level protections; the gate and `next.config.ts` must then change together.
- Public registration admission: the 3C-1 global pool (30/hour, 100/day) is a controlled-development limit that anonymous traffic can exhaust; trusted-ingress per-source or edge protection, and applying its migration to the application database, remain undecided and unauthorized (see "Public Readiness Milestone 3C — Slice 3C-1")
- Applying the 3C-2 marker-table migration to the application database, and real-device validation of the 58-byte stateless login challenge, remain undecided and unauthorized (see "Slice 3C-2")
- Real-device validation of the 64 KiB WebAuthn registration limit (representative Apple, Google, Windows Hello and roaming-key passkeys across attestation formats), and the production hosting, CDN and protocol behavior of the length gate.
- Whether and how to address the bounded challenge purge's plan-dependent scan cost (production-scale latency, plan stability, dead tuples and contention are unmeasured; the SQL was deliberately not changed), the compatibility of the legacy S4 smoke with the 64-row cap, and disposal of the spent proof branch (see "Bounded Shared WebAuthn Challenge Purge")
- Anything in DESIGN.md's own "Deliberately Undecided" list (exact colors, typography, icon style, dark mode default)

These should be decided when they're actually needed to move the project forward, with a preference for the smallest, most boring option that works — and recorded here once chosen.

## How to Use This Document

Update this file to reflect real decisions as they're made (with brief rationale), and keep the boundaries section honest — if a seam stops being real, or a candidate is verified or rejected, say so here rather than leaving stale aspirational architecture on record.
